In the aftermath, she threw herself into supporting the staff, giving statements to police, and designing new safety protocols, while privately trying to keep up her usual commitments to mentoring, salsa class, and church activities as if nothing had changed. Over the following year, she started quietly avoiding evening events in the neighborhood where the robbery took place, became more reluctant to drive alone at night, and found that her enjoyment of museum visits and jazz concerts was increasingly overshadowed by a sense of dread in crowded or unfamiliar spaces, which began to interfere with her work travel and community leadership roles. When she turned down a speaking invitation for a women’s leadership conference because it was scheduled for an evening in a downtown venue and realized she was arranging her life around not feeling safe, she discussed it with a close friend from her business network, who suggested she see a psychiatrist through her primary care clinic.\n- Chief Complaint: “Ever since that robbery, I keep organizing my whole life around avoiding anything that reminds me of it—certain neighborhoods, late meetings, even some community events.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): Most nights I lie there for an hour or more with my mind replaying the day and that night at the store, and when I finally do fall asleep I wake up two or three times, heart racing, and feel like I haven’t really rested at all when the alarm goes off at six.\n- S069 (Intrusive_Symptoms): Out of nowhere—like when I’m locking up an office or I hear a loud male voice behind me—I’ll suddenly see myself back on that floor with the gun at my head, and for a few seconds it feels so real that my chest tightens and I can’t catch my breath.\n- S070 (Trauma_Avoidance): I’ve started scheduling all my client visits for mornings or early afternoons, avoiding that whole part of town after dark, and I make excuses to skip evening church events or networking mixers if they’re in busy downtown areas, because just the thought of being in a store or parking garage at night makes my skin crawl.\n- S071 (Trauma_Related_Amnesia): I can remember lying on the floor and hearing the kid crying and the shouting, but there are chunks of that night—like what exactly the robber said to me or how long it lasted—that are just blank no matter how hard I try to piece it together.\n- S072 (Negative_Beliefs): Since the robbery, I catch myself thinking, ‘You were stupid to be there that late, you should’ve known better,’ and more generally that the world’s just gotten ugly and dangerous and you can’t really let your guard down anywhere anymore.\n- S075 (Social_Detachment): Even when I’m at church or at a family barbecue, I feel like I’m watching everybody from behind glass, nodding and smiling in the right places but not really feeling like I’m part of it or that anyone truly gets what’s going on with me.\n- S076 (Inability_Positive_Emotions): Things that used to light me up—like planning a big sales meeting, going to salsa class, or taking my granddaughter to the museum—now just feel flat, like I’m going through the motions because I’m supposed to enjoy them but nothing really reaches me.\n- S077 (Irritability_Aggression): I find myself snapping at my staff or my sister over little things, like someone being five minutes late or misplacing a file, and I hear the sharpness in my own voice but it’s like the anger is already out before I can pull it back.\n- S079 (Hypervigilance): Wherever I go now, I’m automatically checking who’s coming in and out, where the exits are, and what kind of people are hanging around, and I have a hard time relaxing at a restaurant or store because a part of me is always on watch.\n- S080 (Exaggerated_Startle_Response): If someone comes up behind me at work or a car door slams nearby, I jump out of my skin—my heart just takes off, my whole body tenses, and it takes several minutes before I can shake off that jolt and focus again.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: In the last year I’ve turned down promotions, evening speaking gigs, and most downtown events, and I’ve pulled back from mentoring and church activities so much that my coworkers and friends have started asking why I’m not showing up like I used to.\n- duration: These problems have been hanging over me pretty steadily since a few months after the robbery, so it’s been going on for well over a year now.\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer based on the profile, set matched=true, list related\n In the aftermath, she threw herself into supporting the staff, giving statements to police, and designing new safety protocols, while privately trying to keep up her usual commitments to mentoring, salsa class, and church activities as if nothing had changed. Over the following year, she started quietly avoiding evening events in the neighborhood where the robbery took place, became more reluctant to drive alone at night, and found that her enjoyment of museum visits and jazz concerts was increasingly overshadowed by a sense of dread in crowded or unfamiliar spaces, which began to interfere with her work travel and community leadership roles. When she turned down a speaking invitation for a women’s leadership conference because it was scheduled for an evening in a downtown venue and realized she was arranging her life around not feeling safe, she discussed it with a close friend from her business network, who suggested she see a psychiatrist through her primary care clinic.\n- Chief Complaint: “Ever since that robbery, I keep organizing my whole life around avoiding anything that reminds me of it—certain neighborhoods, late meetings, even some community events.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): Most nights I lie there for an hour or more with my mind replaying the day and that night at the store, and when I finally do fall asleep I wake up two or three times, heart racing, and feel like I haven’t really rested at all when the alarm goes off at six.\n- S069 (Intrusive_Symptoms): Out of nowhere—like when I’m locking up an office or I hear a loud male voice behind me—I’ll suddenly see myself back on that floor with the gun at my head, and for a few seconds it feels so real that my chest tightens and I can’t catch my breath.\n- S070 (Trauma_Avoidance): I’ve kid crying and the shouting, but there are chunks of that night—like what exactly the robber said to me or how long it lasted—that are just blank no matter how hard I try to piece it together.\n- S072 (Negative_Beliefs): Since the robbery, I catch myself thinking, ‘You were stupid to be there that late, you should’ve known better,’ and more generally that the world’s just gotten ugly and dangerous and you can’t really let your guard down anywhere anymore.\n- S075 (Social_Detachment): Even when I’m at church or at a family barbecue, I feel like I’m watching everybody from behind glass, nodding up—like planning a big sales meeting, going to salsa class, or taking my granddaughter to the museum—now just feel flat, like I’m going through the motions because I’m supposed to enjoy them but nothing really reaches me.\n- S077 (Irritability_Aggression): I find myself snapping at my staff or my sister over little things, like someone being five minutes late or misplacing a file, and I hear the sharpness in my own voice but it’s like the anger is already out before I can pull it back.\n- S079 (Hypervigilance): Wherever I go now, I’m automatically checking who’s coming in and out, where the exits are, and what kind of people are hanging around, and I have a hard time relaxing at a restaurant or store because a part of me is always on watch.\n- S080 (Exaggerated_Startle_Response): If organizing my whole life around avoiding anything that reminds me of it—certain neighborhoods, late meetings, even some community events.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): Most nights I lie there for an hour or more with my mind replaying the day and that night at the store, and when I finally do fall asleep I wake up two or three times, heart racing, and feel like I haven’t really rested at all when the alarm goes off at six.\n- S069 (Intrusive_Symptoms): Out of nowhere—like when I’m locking up an office or I hear a loud male voice behind me—I’ll suddenly see myself back on that floor with the gun at my head, and for a few seconds it feels so real that my chest tightens and I can’t catch my breath.\n- S070 (Trauma_Avoidance): I’ve started scheduling all my client visits for mornings or early afternoons, avoiding that whole part of town after dark, and I make excuses to skip evening church events or networking mixers if they’re in busy downtown areas, because just the thought of being in a store or parking garage at night makes my skin crawl.\n- S071 (Trauma_Related_Amnesia): I can remember lying on the floor and hearing the kid crying and the shouting, but there are chunks of that night—like what exactly the robber said to me or how long it lasted—that are just blank no matter how hard I try to piece it together.\n- S072 (Negative_Beliefs): Since the robbery, I catch myself thinking, ‘You were stupid to be there that late, you should’ve known better,’ and more generally that the world’s just gotten ugly and dangerous and you can’t really let your guard down anywhere anymore.\n- S075 (Social_Detachment): Even when I’m at church or at a family barbecue, I feel like I’m watching everybody from behind glass, nodding and smiling in the right places but not really feeling like I’m part of it or that anyone truly gets what’s going on with me.\n- S076 (Inability_Positive_Emotions): Things that used to light me up—like planning a big sales meeting, going to salsa class, or taking my granddaughter to the museum—now just feel flat, like I’m going through the motions because I’m supposed to enjoy them but nothing really reaches me.\n- S077 (Irritability_Aggression): I find myself snapping at my staff or my sister over little things, like someone being five minutes late or misplacing a file, and I hear the sharpness in my own voice but it’s like the anger is already out before I can pull it back.\n- S079 (Hypervigilance): Wherever I go now, I’m automatically checking who’s coming in and out, where the exits are, and what kind of people are hanging around, and I have a hard time relaxing at a restaurant or store because a part of me is always on watch.\n- S080 (Exaggerated_Startle_Response): If someone comes up behind me at work or a car door slams nearby, I jump out of my skin—my heart just takes off, my whole body tenses, and it takes several minutes before I can shake off that jolt and focus again.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: In the last year I’ve turned down promotions, evening speaking gigs, and most downtown events, and I’ve pulled back from mentoring and church activities so much that my coworkers and friends have started asking why I’m not showing up like I used to.\n- duration: These problems have been hanging over me pretty steadily What brings you in to see me?\n\nPatient: Ever since that robbery, I keep organizing my whole life around avoiding anything that reminds me of it—certain neighborhoods, late meetings, even some community events. It’s gotten to the point where I realize I'm arranging my entire schedule just to feel safe, and a close friend suggested I finally come speak to someone.\n\nDoctor: Since the robbery, do you also have nightmares, flashbacks, or sudden intense distress when something reminds you of it? In the aftermath, she threw herself into supporting the staff, giving statements to police, and designing new safety protocols, while privately trying to keep up her usual commitments to mentoring, salsa class, and church activities as if nothing had changed. Over the following year, she started quietly avoiding evening events in the neighborhood where the robbery took place, became more reluctant to drive alone at night, and found that her enjoyment of museum visits and jazz concerts was increasingly overshadowed by a sense of dread in crowded or unfamiliar spaces, which began to interfere with her work travel and community leadership roles. When she turned down a speaking invitation for a women’s leadership conference because it was scheduled for an evening in a downtown venue and realized she was arranging her life around not feeling safe, she discussed it with a close friend from her business network, who suggested she see a psychiatrist through her primary care clinic.\n- Chief Complaint: “Ever since that robbery, I keep organizing my whole life around avoiding anything that reminds me of it—certain neighborhoods, late meetings, even some community events.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): Most nights I lie there for an hour or more with my mind replaying the day and that night at the store, and when I finally do fall asleep I wake up two or three times, heart racing, and feel like I haven’t really rested at all when the alarm goes off at six.\n- S069 (Intrusive_Symptoms): Out of nowhere—like when I’m locking up an office or I hear a loud male voice behind me—I’ll suddenly see myself back on that floor with the gun at my head, and for a few seconds it feels so real that my chest tightens and I can’t catch my breath.\n- S070 (Trauma_Avoidance): I’ve started scheduling all my client visits for mornings or early afternoons, avoiding that whole part of town after dark, and I make excuses to skip evening church events or networking mixers if they’re in busy downtown areas, because just the thought of being in a store or parking garage at night makes my skin crawl.\n- S071 (Trauma_Related_Amnesia): I can remember lying on the floor and hearing the kid crying and the shouting, but there are chunks of that night—like what exactly the robber said to me or how long it lasted—that are just blank no matter how hard I try to piece it together.\n- S072 (Negative_Beliefs): Since the robbery, I catch myself thinking, ‘You were stupid to be there that late, you should’ve known better,’ and more generally that the world’s just gotten ugly and dangerous and you can’t really let your guard down anywhere anymore.\n- S075 (Social_Detachment): Even when I’m at church or at a family barbecue, I feel like I’m watching everybody from behind glass, nodding and smiling in the right places but not really feeling like I’m part of it or that anyone truly gets what’s going on with me.\n- S076 (Inability_Positive_Emotions): Things that used to light me up—like planning a big sales meeting, going to salsa class, or taking my granddaughter to the museum—now just feel flat, like I’m going through the motions because I’m supposed to enjoy them but nothing really reaches me.\n- S077 (Irritability_Aggression): I find myself snapping at my staff or my sister over little things, like someone being five minutes late or misplacing a file, and I hear the sharpness in my own voice but it’s like the anger is already out before I can pull it back.\n- S079 (Hypervigilance): Wherever I go now, I’m automatically checking who’s coming in and out, where the exits are, and what kind of people are hanging around, and I have a hard time relaxing at a restaurant or store because a part of me is always on watch.\n- S080 (Exaggerated_Startle_Response): If someone comes up behind me at work or a car door slams nearby, I jump out of my skin—my heart just takes off, my whole body tenses, and it takes several minutes before I can shake off that jolt and focus again.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: In the last year I’ve turned down promotions, evening speaking gigs, and most downtown events, and I’ve pulled back from mentoring and church activities so much that my coworkers and friends have started asking why I’m not showing up like I used to.\n- duration: These problems have been hanging over me pretty steadily safe, she discussed it with a close friend from her business network, who suggested she see a psychiatrist through her primary care clinic.\n- Chief Complaint: “Ever since that robbery, I keep organizing my whole life around avoiding anything that reminds me of it—certain neighborhoods, late meetings, even some community events.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): Most nights I lie there for an hour or more with my mind replaying the day and that night at the store, and when I finally do fall asleep I wake up two or three times, heart racing, and feel like I haven’t really rested at all when the alarm goes off at six.\n- S069 (Intrusive_Symptoms): Out of nowhere—like when I’m locking up an office or I hear a loud male voice behind me—I’ll suddenly see myself back on that floor with the gun at my head, and for a few seconds it feels so real that my chest tightens and I can’t catch my breath.\n- S070 (Trauma_Avoidance): I’ve started scheduling all my client visits for mornings or early afternoons, avoiding that whole part of town after dark, and I make excuses to skip evening church events or networking mixers if they’re in busy downtown areas, because just the thought of being in a store or parking garage at night makes my skin crawl.\n- S071 (Trauma_Related_Amnesia): I can remember lying on the floor and hearing the kid crying and the shouting, but there are chunks of that night—like what exactly the robber said to me or how long it lasted—that are just blank no matter how hard I try to piece it together.\n- S072 (Negative_Beliefs): Since the robbery, I catch myself thinking, ‘You were stupid to be there that late, you should’ve known better,’ and more generally that the world’s just gotten ugly and dangerous and you can’t really let your guard down anywhere anymore.\n- S075 (Social_Detachment): Even when I’m at church or at a family barbecue, I feel like I’m watching everybody from behind glass, nodding and smiling in the right places but not really feeling like I’m part of it or that anyone truly gets what’s going on with me.\n- S076 (Inability_Positive_Emotions): Things that used to light me up—like planning a big sales meeting, going to salsa class, or taking my granddaughter to the museum—now just feel flat, like I’m going through the motions because I’m supposed to enjoy them but nothing really reaches me.\n- S077 (Irritability_Aggression): I find myself snapping at my staff or my sister over little things, like someone being five minutes late or misplacing a file, and I hear the sharpness in my own voice but it’s like the anger is already out before I can pull it back.\n- S079 (Hypervigilance): Wherever I go now, I’m automatically checking who’s coming in and out, where the exits are, and what kind of people are hanging around, and I have a hard time relaxing at a restaurant or store because a part of me is always on watch.\n- S080 (Exaggerated_Startle_Response): If someone comes up behind me at work or a car door slams nearby, I jump out of my skin—my heart just takes off, my whole body tenses, and it takes several minutes before I can shake off that jolt and mentoring and church activities so much that my coworkers and friends have started asking why I’m not showing up like I used to.\n- duration: These problems have been hanging over me pretty steadily
